# Item
## id
CLM-BE9F02C96E1F
## concept_id
CON-RES-671ABF9D60BE19
## subject
Each intercostal space holds three layers of muscle from superficial to deep: the external intercostal, running obliquely downwards and forwards from the tubercle of the rib above to the costochondral junction, where it
## predicate
becomes
## object
the anterior intercostal membrane; the internal intercostal, running obliquely downwards and backwards from the sternum to the angle of the rib, continuing as the posterior intercostal membrane; and the innermost intercostal, in the same plane and direction as the internal but separated from it by the neurovascular bundle
## display_text
Each intercostal space holds three layers of muscle from superficial to deep: the external intercostal, running obliquely downwards and forwards from the tubercle of the rib above to the costochondral junction, where it becomes the anterior intercostal membrane; the internal intercostal, running obliquely downwards and backwards from the sternum to the angle of the rib, continuing as the posterior intercostal membrane; and the innermost intercostal, in the same plane and direction as the internal but separated from it by the neurovascular bundle.
## risk_class
foundational_stable
## verification_status
needs_evidence
## conflict_status
none
## confidence
0.4
## freshness
stable_local_curriculum_fact
## time_sensitive
no
## qualifiers
concept: The intercostal space has three muscle layers — external, internal and innermost — of which the external is the main inspiratory elevator of the rib
curriculum: 104 CPS > Anatomy > Intercostal Spaces

# Item
## id
CLM-9FDDBF698E9F
## concept_id
CON-RES-B833146BBFCB26
## subject
The anterior border descends from the sternoclavicular joint to the sternal angle identically on both sides
## predicate
states
## object
The anterior border descends from the sternoclavicular joint to the sternal angle identically on both sides; below that, the right border continues vertically to the xiphisternal junction while the left deviates laterally at the fourth costal cartilage to leave room for the heart before rejoining the sternal margin at the sixth cartilage.
## display_text
The anterior border descends from the sternoclavicular joint to the sternal angle identically on both sides; below that, the right border continues vertically to the xiphisternal junction while the left deviates laterally at the fourth costal cartilage to leave room for the heart before rejoining the sternal margin at the sixth cartilage.
## risk_class
foundational_stable
## verification_status
needs_evidence

0.4
## freshness

## time_sensitive

## subject
The three major openings of the diaphragm
## predicate
are
## object
stacked at three vertebral levels in the midline plane, each transmitting a distinct set of structures: the aortic opening, behind the median arcuate ligament at T12, transmitting the aorta, the azygos vein to its right and the thoracic duct between them; the oesophageal opening, in the right crus at T10 one inch left of the midline, transmitting the oesophagus, the two vagi and the oesophageal branches of the left gastric vessels; and the caval opening, in the central tendon at T8 one inch right of the midline, transmitting the inferior vena cava, the right phrenic nerve and lymphatics
## display_text
The three major openings of the diaphragm are stacked at three vertebral levels in the midline plane, each transmitting a distinct set of structures: the aortic opening, behind the median arcuate ligament at T12, transmitting the aorta, the azygos vein to its right and the thoracic duct between them; the oesophageal opening, in the right crus at T10 one inch left of the midline, transmitting the oesophagus, the two vagi and the oesophageal branches of the left gastric vessels; and the caval opening, in the central tendon at T8 one inch right of the midline, transmitting the inferior vena cava, the right phrenic nerve and lymphatics.

states

# Item
## id

## subject

## predicate
arises
## object
from the left posterior aortic sinus, passes between the pulmonary trunk and left auricle to the top of the anterior interventricular groove, and ends by dividing into the anterior interventricular artery — which descends to the inferior border, turns round it and anastomoses with the right coronary artery's posterior interventricular branch, supplying the sternocostal surface of the left ventricle, a strip of the right ventricle and the anterior two thirds of the septum — and the circumflex artery, which supplies the left atrium and, through its marginal branch, the left ventricle down to the apex, and the SA node in 40% and the AV node in 20% of people
## display_text
The left coronary artery, larger than the right, arises from the left posterior aortic sinus, passes between the pulmonary trunk and left auricle to the top of the anterior interventricular groove, and ends by dividing into the anterior interventricular artery — which descends to the inferior border, turns round it and anastomoses with the right coronary artery's posterior interventricular branch, supplying the sternocostal surface of the left ventricle, a strip of the right ventricle and the anterior two thirds of the septum — and the circumflex artery, which supplies the left atrium and, through its marginal branch, the left ventricle down to the apex, and the SA node in 40% and the AV node in 20% of people.
## risk_class
foundational_stable

needs_evidence

## time_sensitive
no

concept: The left coronary artery divides into the anterior interventricular and circumflex arteries, together supplying the left atrium, most of the left ventricle and the anterior two thirds of the septum

# Item
## id

## subject
The cardiac sling adds hooking: the aortic arch and left pulmonary artery hook around the left principal bronchus
## predicate
states
## object
The cardiac sling adds hooking: the aortic arch and left pulmonary artery hook around the left principal bronchus, the arch of the azygos vein and right pulmonary artery hook around the right principal bronchus, and the subclavian vessels hang across the first rib on each side at the thoracic inlet — together fixing the base of the heart while leaving its ventricular part free to contract.

states

Because it hooks around a thoracic structure — unlike the right recurrent laryngeal nerve

# Item
## id
CLM-B686106E7846
## concept_id
CON-CVS-6BF9AE4B31AD3D
## subject
The thoracic sympathetic chain
## predicate
states
## object
The thoracic sympathetic chain, of eleven or twelve ganglia — the first often fused with the inferior cervical ganglion as the stellate ganglion — continues the cervical chain from in front of the first rib's neck, running in the upper thorax in front of the rib necks and in the lower thorax along the vertebral bodies, the right chain lateral to the azygos vein and the left lateral to the two hemiazygos veins, covered throughout by costal pleura, before passing behind the medial arcuate ligament to become the lumbar chain.
## display_text
The thoracic sympathetic chain, of eleven or twelve ganglia — the first often fused with the inferior cervical ganglion as the stellate ganglion — continues the cervical chain from in front of the first rib's neck, running in the upper thorax in front of the rib necks and in the lower thorax along the vertebral bodies, the right chain lateral to the azygos vein and the left lateral to the two hemiazygos veins, covered throughout by costal pleura, before passing behind the medial arcuate ligament to become the lumbar chain.
## risk_class
foundational_stable
## verification_status
needs_evidence
## conflict_status
none
## confidence
0.4
## freshness
stable_local_curriculum_fact
## time_sensitive
no
## qualifiers
concept: The thoracic sympathetic chain runs on the rib necks above and the vertebral bodies below, giving cardiac and pulmonary branches from its upper ganglia and the three splanchnic nerves from its lower ones
curriculum: 104 CPS > Anatomy > Large Nerves of the Thorax

# Item
## id
CLM-64B49B5A74E1
## concept_id
CON-GIT-4E4EC465826CF2
## subject
The thoracic oesophagus
## predicate
states
## object
The thoracic oesophagus, about 25 cm long overall, enters the thorax through the thoracic inlet between the trachea in front and the vertebral column behind, running as the most posterior structure of the superior mediastinum to the right of the aortic arch; at the sternal angle it passes behind the left principal bronchus into the posterior mediastinum, descending along the descending thoracic aorta's right side, behind the pericardium and the left atrium, to T7, then deviating forwards and left, crossing in front of the aorta, to leave through the diaphragm's oesophageal opening at T10, one inch left of the midline.
## display_text
The thoracic oesophagus, about 25 cm long overall, enters the thorax through the thoracic inlet between the trachea in front and the vertebral column behind, running as the most posterior structure of the superior mediastinum to the right of the aortic arch; at the sternal angle it passes behind the left principal bronchus into the posterior mediastinum, descending along the descending thoracic aorta's right side, behind the pericardium and the left atrium, to T7, then deviating forwards and left, crossing in front of the aorta, to leave through the diaphragm's oesophageal opening at T10, one inch left of the midline.

foundational_stable

needs_evidence

none

stable_local_curriculum_fact

# Item
## id

## subject
It enters the thorax through the aortic opening of the diaphragm between the aorta on its left and the azygos vein on it
## predicate
states
## object
It enters the thorax through the aortic opening of the diaphragm between the aorta on its left and the azygos vein on its right, ascends in the posterior mediastinum behind the oesophagus's right border, crosses behind the oesophagus from right to left at T5, then ascends in the superior mediastinum behind the oesophagus's left border and the aortic arch, ending at the root of the neck by opening into the start of the left brachiocephalic vein at the junction of the left internal jugular and subclavian veins.

Septum secundum then grows down to the right of septum primum

states

# Item
## id
CLM-85B1890C0770
## concept_id
CON-DEV-B33D9F68392311
## subject
At birth
## predicate
states
## object
At birth, respiration expands the lungs and establishes the pulmonary circulation; left atrial pressure rises as pulmonary venous return increases while right atrial pressure falls as placental flow stops, and that reversed pressure gradient presses septum primum against septum secundum, functionally closing the foramen ovale, while the ductus arteriosus closes by contraction of its own muscular wall.
## display_text
At birth, respiration expands the lungs and establishes the pulmonary circulation; left atrial pressure rises as pulmonary venous return increases while right atrial pressure falls as placental flow stops, and that reversed pressure gradient presses septum primum against septum secundum, functionally closing the foramen ovale, while the ductus arteriosus closes by contraction of its own muscular wall.
## risk_class
foundational_stable
## verification_status
needs_evidence
## conflict_status
none
## confidence
0.4
## freshness
stable_local_curriculum_fact
## time_sensitive
no

curriculum: 104 CPS > Anatomy > Development of the Heart

# Item
## id
CLM-25DE71D693A7
## concept_id
CON-RES-7C79F2D68F1003
## subject
Bronchioles go further: simple columnar or cuboidal ciliated epithelium with Clara cells and no goblet cells
## predicate
states
## object
Bronchioles go further: simple columnar or cuboidal ciliated epithelium with Clara cells and no goblet cells, cilia disappearing as the bronchiole narrows; more developed, circularly arranged smooth muscle whose contraction now controls airflow to the gas-exchange sites rather than merely shortening the airway; and no cartilage plate, mucoserous gland or lymphatic nodule at all.
## display_text

# Item
## id

## concept_id
CON-RES-7C79F2D68F1003
## subject
Clara cells — dome-shaped
## predicate
states
## object
Clara cells — dome-shaped, non-ciliated, rich in basal rough endoplasmic reticulum, apical smooth endoplasmic reticulum, mitochondria, Golgi apparatus and glycoprotein-containing secretory granules — protect the bronchiolar epithelium, degrade inhaled toxins, secrete a surfactant-like material that keeps the bronchiole patent, protect against emphysema by inhibiting macrophage-secreted protease and elastase, and may act as stem cells for the bronchiolar lining.
## display_text

## subject
The interalveolar septa in which this barrier sits
## predicate
are
## object
delicate partitions of loose connective tissue between adjacent alveoli, containing the richest capillary network in the body, elastic fibres allowing lung expansion on inspiration, reticular fibres supporting the tissue and preventing overexpansion that would injure the capillaries, and extravasated leucocytes, especially monocytes, that become alveolar phagocytes
## display_text
The interalveolar septa in which this barrier sits are delicate partitions of loose connective tissue between adjacent alveoli, containing the richest capillary network in the body, elastic fibres allowing lung expansion on inspiration, reticular fibres supporting the tissue and preventing overexpansion that would injure the capillaries, and extravasated leucocytes, especially monocytes, that become alveolar phagocytes.

states
## object

# Item
## id
CLM-83E55C63C54F
## concept_id
CON-FND-685D573458A6D7
## subject
The first meiotic division (reduction division)
## predicate
has
## object
a prophase I that is long — 22 days in spermatogenesis, 12 to 45 years in oogenesis — passing through stages in which the 46 d-chromosomes appear as long threads, then pair as 23 bivalents of one maternal and one paternal homologue each, then condense into tetrads of four chromatids in which crossing over exchanges segments between non-sister chromatids at the chiasmata (with the help of recombinase), before the nucleolus and nuclear envelope disappear; metaphase I aligns the 23 bivalent d-chromosomes on the spindle; anaphase I separates the bivalents, one homologous d-chromosome to each pole; and telophase I yields two daughter cells, each with 23 d-chromosomes
## display_text
The first meiotic division (reduction division) has a prophase I that is long — 22 days in spermatogenesis, 12 to 45 years in oogenesis — passing through stages in which the 46 d-chromosomes appear as long threads, then pair as 23 bivalents of one maternal and one paternal homologue each, then condense into tetrads of four chromatids in which crossing over exchanges segments between non-sister chromatids at the chiasmata (with the help of recombinase), before the nucleolus and nuclear envelope disappear; metaphase I aligns the 23 bivalent d-chromosomes on the spindle; anaphase I separates the bivalents, one homologous d-chromosome to each pole; and telophase I yields two daughter cells, each with 23 d-chromosomes.

# Item
## id
CLM-A0DE251EB8D8
## concept_id
CON-FND-685D573458A6D7
## subject
The second meiotic division (equatorial division)
## predicate
is
## object
mitosis-like, follows rapidly with a very short interphase and no S-phase: prophase II shortens the chromosomes and forms a new spindle; metaphase II aligns the 23 d-chromosomes at the equator; anaphase II splits each d-chromosome at its centromere into two chromatids (s-chromosomes) moving to opposite poles; and telophase II separates the two daughter cells, each now with 23 s-chromosomes — the haploid number
## display_text
The second meiotic division (equatorial division) is mitosis-like, follows rapidly with a very short interphase and no S-phase: prophase II shortens the chromosomes and forms a new spindle; metaphase II aligns the 23 d-chromosomes at the equator; anaphase II splits each d-chromosome at its centromere into two chromatids (s-chromosomes) moving to opposite poles; and telophase II separates the two daughter cells, each now with 23 s-chromosomes — the haploid number.
